drugs (e.g., switch from aromatase inhibitor (AI) to fulvestrant for hormone
receptor positive (HR)-positive disease).
The NCCN panel has initially stratified patients with recurrent or stage IV
breast cancer at diagnosis initially according to whether bone metastases
are present and then further stratified by tumor HR and HER2 status.
Therapy for Bone Metastases
Complications from bone metastases include pain, decreased
performance status, and decreased quality of life (QOL), as well as
skeletal-related events (SREs), which are defined as the need for radiation
or surgery to bone, pathologic fractures, spinal cord compression, and
hypercalcemia of malignancy.
Bisphosphonates
There are extensive data from randomized trials in support of the use of
bisphosphonates for patients with metastatic disease to bone. The
randomized clinical trial data include the use of zoledronic acid and
pamidronate in the United States and ibandronate and clodronate in
European countries.479-486 In metastatic bone disease, bisphosphonate
treatment is associated with fewer SREs, fewer pathologic fractures, and
less need for radiation therapy (RT) and surgery to treat bone pain. The
use of bisphosphonates in metastatic disease is a palliative care
measure. No impact on overall survival (OS) has been observed in
patients treated with bisphosphonates.
Three randomized trials have compared zoledronic acid dosed every 4
weeks versus every 12 weeks.487-489 Data from these trials show that
among patients with breast cancer and bone metastases, zoledronic acid
administered once every 12 weeks versus once every 4 weeks does not
compromise efficacy and has similar rates of SREs.487,488,490 In the
ZOOM trial,487 the rate of skeletal morbidities was 0.22 (95% CI, 0.14–
0.29) in those receiving zoledronic acid every 4 weeks versus 0.26 (95%
CI, 0.15–0.37) in those receiving zoledronic acid every 12 weeks. In the
CALGB 70604 trial,488 the rate of SREs in the 4-week arm was 29.5%
This section for Management of Recurrent or Stage IV (M1) Disease was
updated on May 8th, 2026

# MS-60

<!-- ===== Page 187 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-60
versus 28.6% in the 12-week arm. In the OPTIMIZE-2 trial,489 the rate of
SREs was 22% in the 4-week arm and 23.2% in the 12-week arm.489 The
NCCN Panel recommends an optimal dosing of every 12 weeks.
The use of bisphosphonates should be accompanied by calcium and
vitamin D supplementation with daily doses of calcium of 1200 to 1500
mg and vitamin D3 of 400 to 800 IU. The dose is individualized and
adjusted upward when patients are found to be vitamin D deficient or
insufficient. Vitamin D status should be assessed before initiating
bisphosphonate therapy and periodically during treatment to ensure
therapeutic adequacy and patient safety. The original studies continued
treatment for up to 24 months; however, there are limited long-term
safety data indicating treatment can continue beyond that time.482,484,491
The risk of renal toxicity necessitates monitoring of serum creatinine prior
to administration of each dose and dose reduction or discontinuation if
renal function is reduced.
Denosumab
Patients who are candidates for bisphosphonate therapy may also be
considered for treatment with denosumab. This recommendation is
based on the results of a randomized trial comparing 120 mg of SQ
denosumab every 4 weeks to 4 mg of IV zoledronic acid every 4
weeks.492 In this trial with noninferiority as the primary endpoint,
denosumab was shown to significantly delay time to first SRE by 18% as
compared with zoledronic acid (HR, 0.82; 95% CI, 0.71–0.95; P < .001
for noninferiority; P = .01 for superiority) and time to first and subsequent
SREs (rate ratio, 0.77; 95% CI, 0.66–0.89; P = .001). No difference in
time to progression or OS was observed.492 Dosing of denosumab
outside of every 3 to 6 weeks has not been studied. Since denosumab
inhibits bone resorption, patients are at high risk for hypocalcemia.
Calcium and vitamin D supplementation should be initiated before the
first dose of denosumab and continued throughout therapy.
The NCCN Panel recommends treatment with a bone-modifying agent
such as zoledronic acid, pamidronate, or denosumab (category 1) in
addition to cytotoxic therapy and/or endocrine therapy if bone metastasis
is present, the expected survival is ≥3 months, and renal function is
adequate. Patients should undergo a dental examination with preventive
dentistry prior to initiation of this therapy. The bisphosphonates and
denosumab are associated with a risk of development of osteonecrosis of
the jaw (ONJ). Poor baseline dental health or dental procedures during
treatment are known risk factors for ONJ. Thus, a dental examination with
preventive dental intervention is recommended prior to treatment with IV
bisphosphonate or denosumab. During treatment, it is recommended to
avoid invasive dental procedures if at all possible. Additional risk factors
for the development of ONJ include administration of chemotherapy or
corticosteroids and poor oral hygiene with periodontal disease and dental
abscess.493
Endocrine-based Therapy for HR-Positive, HER2-Negative Breast Cancer
Patients with HR-positive, HER2-negative tumors are treated with
endocrine-based therapy as backbone in combination with CDK4/6
inhibitors as the preferred first-line approach in those with no visceral
crisis or in those with extensive visceral metastases. The choice of
endocrine therapy depends on prior adjuvant endocrine agent used,
disease-free interval, and patient preference. Duration of initial response
to endocrine therapy can guide likelihood of benefit of continued
endocrine therapy as later line therapy. Patients whose disease
progresses after a year from the end of adjuvant endocrine-based
therapy or those who present with de novo stage IV/metastatic breast
cancer are eligible for first-line aromatase inhibitor (AI) therapy in
combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor.

# MS-61

<!-- ===== Page 188 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-61
Visceral: Cytotoxic therapy is reserved for visceral crisis and/or endocrine-
refractory disease. Visceral crisis as per the ESO-ESMO international
consensus guidelines494 is defined as “severe organ dysfunction, as
assessed by signs and symptoms, laboratory studies, and rapid
progression of disease. Visceral crisis is not the mere presence of visceral
metastases but implies important organ compromise leading to a clinical
indication for the most rapidly efficacious therapy.”494,495
Extensive visceral metastases: The Right Choice randomized, open-
label, multi-center phase II study compared ribociclib + endocrine
therapy with chemotherapy in premenopausal or perimenopausal
patients with locally advanced or metastatic HR-positive, HER2-negative
breast cancer.496 Patients treated with the ribociclib combination in the
trial had a longer progression-free survival (PFS) compared with those
who received the chemotherapy regimen (median PFS, 21.8 vs 12.8
months; hazard ratio [HR], 0.61; 95% CI, 0.43–0.87) and had fewer
grade 3–4 adverse events (0.9% vs 7.0%).496 However, overall response
rates (ORR) (66.1% vs 61.8%) and time to tumor response was not
statistically significant (4.9 vs 3.2 months) between the ribociclib-
containing versus chemotherapy arms.496 The final results of this trial,
reported that median OS was not reached in either group, however, no
meaningful difference in survival benefit was seen between the two
arms. At 30-month follow-up, the OS rates were 67% in the ribociclib +
endocrine therapy arm and compared with 65% in the chemotherapy
arm, respectively (HR, 0.92; 95% CI 0.56–1.52).496 It is important to note
that the key limitations of this trial were size of the trial (small phase II),
open-label design, and a selective, non-anthracycline chemotherapy
control arm. In addition, it focused on pre/perimenopausal patients and
relied on investigator-defined visceral crisis. Two additional ongoing
phase III PADMA and AMBRE trials have studied CDK4/6 inhibitors
combined with hormone therapy compared with chemotherapy for
patients with aggressive, HR-positive metastatic breast cancer. The
preliminary results of both these studies presented at the 2025 SABCS
showed improved PFS in high-risk cases, with AMBRE trial showing
benefit in patients with high-visceral burden.497,498
The NCCN Panel has noted in a footnote that endocrine therapy
combinations with targeted therapies (doublet with CDK4/6 inhibitors or
triplets with CDK4/6 inhibitors and PIK3CA inhibitors) may be considered
in certain circumstances with visceral crisis.
The NCCN Panel has listed endocrine-based therapies useful in the
first-line versus second- and subsequent-line settings. The NCCN Panel
recommends that premenopausal patients with HR-positive disease
should have adequate ovarian suppression/ablation and then be treated
similarly to postmenopausal patients.
Preferred First-Line Therapy Options for HR-Positive, HER2-Negative
Breast Cancer
AI in combination with CDK4/6 inhibitor: In postmenopausal or
premenopausal patients receiving ovarian ablation or ovarian function
suppression, combinations of AIs with CDK4/6 inhibitors (palbociclib,
ribociclib, or abemaciclib) have demonstrated improved PFS (and in
some cases, eg, ribociclib, an OS benefit) relative to an AI alone.
However, the CDK4/6 inhibitors have not been directly compared in
clinical trials.
Ribociclib: Ribociclib in combination with letrozole was also studied as
first-line therapy in a phase III MONALEESA-2 study of postmenopausal
patients (n = 668) with HR-positive, HER2-negative recurrent/stage IV
breast cancer. At a median follow-up of 26.4 months, an improvement in
PFS (25.3 vs 16.0 months; HR for progression or death was 0.56; 95%
CI, 0.45–0.70) and improved ORR of 43% versus 29% was seen with
ribociclib + letrozole compared with letrozole alone.499 All-grade adverse
events were reported in 85 (25.4%) in patients receiving ribociclib +

# MS-62

<!-- ===== Page 189 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-62
letrozole versus 51 (15.5%) in those receiving placebo + letrozole; the
most common (≥1.5%) were pneumonia (1.8% versus 0.9%), abdominal
pain (1.5% versus 0%), dyspnea (1.5% versus 0.6%), and vomiting
(1.5% versus 0.6%). Grade 3 or 4 adverse events were more common
with the combination and included neutropenia (62% vs 1.2%),
leukopenia (21.3% vs 0.9%), and abnormal liver function tests (LFTs)
(10.2% vs 2.4%).499  A total of 12 patients (3.6%) receiving ribociclib +
letrozole had at least one postbaseline QTcF of>480 msec compared
with 2 patients (0.6%)  receiving placebo + Letrozole. Based on these QT
prolongation observations, the prescribing information for ribociclib
includes careful selection of patients with QTcF values<450 msec.500
The phase III MONALEESA-7 trial further confirmed the results of
MONALEESA-2 that ribociclib + endocrine therapy significantly improved
PFS (23.8 vs 13.0 months). While most trials studying CDK4/6 inhibitor
with an AI have mainly included postmenopausal patients and only a
small subset of premenopausal patients on ovarian suppression,
MONALEESA-7 trial addressed this gap. In this trial, pre- or
perimenopausal patients (n = 672) with HR-positive, HER2-negative
advanced breast cancer randomly assigned to first-line treatment with
ribociclib or placebo with goserelin + either a nonsteroidal AI or
tamoxifen.501 Grade 3 and 4 adverse events reported in >10% of patients
in either group were consistent with MONALEESA-2 trial (neutropenia,
61% vs 4% and leukopenia, 14% vs 1%).501
Importantly, ribociclib + endocrine therapy has demonstrated a clear OS
benefit in both MONALEESA-2 (63.9 vs 51.4 months; HR, 0.76; 95% CI,
0.63–0.93) 502 and MONALEESA-7 trials (58.7 vs 48.0 months; HR, 0.76;
95% CI, 0.61–0.96).503
Abemaciclib: The phase III MONARCH3 trial studied the combination of
abemaciclib with either an AI (letrozole or anastrozole) or AI
monotherapy as first-line treatment of patients with advanced
HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer. The combination of
abemaciclib with the AI improved PFS, compared with the AI alone
(median not reached vs 14.7 months, respectively; HR, 0.54; 95% CI,
0.41–0.72).504 The ORR was higher with the combination compared with
AI monotherapy (59% vs 44 %).504 The most frequent grade ≥3 adverse
events for abemaciclib versus placebo included diarrhea (9.5% vs1.2%),
neutropenia (21.1% vs 1.2%), leukopenia (8% vs 0.6%), and fatigue (2%
vs 0%).504 ILD/pneumonitis was reported in 17 (5.2%) patients with ≤1%
of cases grade ≥3. Laboratory‐based abnormalities of increased
creatinine (all grades) were observed in 98% of patients and 85%
experienced some degree of diarrhea. The final OS analysis with 8.1
years of median follow-up showed a trend towards longer OS with the
addition of abemaciclib to AI (median, 66.8 vs 53.7 months; HR, 0.80;
95% CI, 0.64–1.02).505 While the difference of 13 months is considered
clinically significant, the analysis showed no statistically significant
difference in OS between the two arms.
Palbociclib: Palbociclib in combination with letrozole was studied in a
phase III study that included postmenopausal patients (n = 666) with
metastatic, HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer who had not
received prior treatment for advanced disease.506 An improvement in
PFS (24.8 vs 14.5 months; HR, 0.58; 95% CI, 0.46–0.72) and objective
response rate (ORR; 42% vs 35%) was seen with the combination of
palbociclib and letrozole compared with letrozole alone.506 Grade 3 and 4
adverse effects seen with the combination of palbociclib and letrozole
included neutropenia (66.5% vs 1.4%), leukopenia (24.8% vs 0%),
anemia (5.4% vs 1.8%), and fatigue (1.8% vs 0.5%).506 Final analysis of
the PALOMA-2 trial (90.1-month median follow-up) showed no
statistically significant difference in median OS between the combination
versus letrozole alone (53.9 vs 51.2 months; HR, 0.96; 95% CI, 0.78–
1.2).507 A pre-planned pooled analysis of patients from PALOMA-1 trial508
(phase II proof of concept trial) and PALOMA-2 reinforced this finding-

# MS-63

<!-- ===== Page 190 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-63
the median OS was not statistically significant with 51.8 months in the
palbociclib combination group compared to 46.8 months in the control
arm (HR, 0.92; 95% CI, 0.78–1.12).
Based on the above data, the NCCN Panel has included CDK4/6
inhibitors in combination with AI as preferred first-line options for
postmenopausal and premenopausal patients with ovarian
ablation/suppression with HR-positive, HER2-negative recurrent/stage IV
breast cancer. Due to the clear OS benefit seen with ribociclib in
combination with AI, it is a category 1 recommendation. Abemaciclib and
palbociclib are both category 2A recommendations.
The phase 3 SONIA trial randomized patients (n=1,050) who had not
received previous therapy for advanced breast cancer to receive CDK4/6
inhibitor in the first- or second-line setting.509 All the patients received an
AI  for first-line treatment and fulvestrant for second-line treatment. The
primary end point was defined as the time from randomization to disease
progression after second-line treatment (progression-free survival 2
(PFS2)). No statistically significant difference in PFS2 was seen between
using CDK4/6 inhibitors in the first-line versus second-line setting for HR-
positive, HER2-negative advanced breast cancer (median, 31.0 vs. 26.8
months, respectively; HR = 0.87; 95% confidence interval = 0.74-1.03;
P = .10). While first-line use resulted in longer treatment duration and
more adverse events, health-related quality of life was similar across
both groups, suggesting that deferring CDK4/6i to second-line treatment
is a viable strategy. Key limitations of this study are that approximately
half of enrolled patients had prior endocrine therapy with 64% of those
patients having disease progression >2 years since end of prior
endocrine therapy. In addition, 35% of patients had de novo metastatic
disease. Therefore, this may have been an endocrine sensitive patient
population. Palbociclib was also the planned CDK4/6 inhibitor in >90% of
the participants 509
For disease progression on adjuvant endocrine therapy or with relapsed
disease within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy completion, the
Panel had listed the following options:
Fulvestrant/Inavolisib/Palbociclib: For patients with PIK3CA-activating
mutations, fulvestrant/inavolisib/palbociclib is listed as a category 1,
preferred option based on the data from the INAVO120 trial. In this trial,
patients (n = 325 ) with PIK3CA-activating mutations with recurrence on
or within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy, were randomized to
receive treatment with fulvestrant/inavolisib/palbociclib (n =161) versus
fulvestrant/placebo/palbociclib (n = 165). The addition of inavolisib,
significantly improved PFS (15.0 vs 7.3 months; HR, 0.43; 95% CI, 0.32–
0.59).510 The median OS was also significantly improved with the
addition of inavolisib (34 vs 27 months; HR, 0.67; 95% CI, 0.48–0.94).511
The incidence of adverse events such as neutropenia,
thrombocytopenia, leukopenia, anemia, hyperglycemia, stomatitis,
diarrhea, and ocular toxic effects were higher with inavolisib compared
with the placebo arm. A key limitation of the trial is the lack of cross-over
which is relevant as PIK3CA-pathway inhibitors are known to have
significant activity in later line settings. Thus, it is unknown whether
treatment with inavolisib in the first- line versus in a later line is truly
superior.
Fulvestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor: Fulvestrant in combination with a CDK4/6
inhibitor may be offered to patients who experienced disease
progression on adjuvant endocrine therapy or relapse within 12 months
of adjuvant endocrine therapy completion.
In the phase III trial, MONALEESA-3, in patients (n = 726) with advanced
HR-positive breast cancer who had no prior endocrine therapy or had
disease progression on prior therapy, the combination of ribociclib with
fulvestrant showed improved PFS versus fulvestrant alone (21 vs 13
months; HR, 0.59; 95% CI, 0.48–0.73).512 The PFS benefits were

# MS-64

<!-- ===== Page 191 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-64
consistent across patients with and without prior endocrine treatment. In
a subsequent analysis, a significant improvement in OS was observed.513
At 42 months the estimated OS was 57.8% (95% CI, 52.0–63.2) in the
ribociclib group and 45.9% (95% CI, 36.9–54.5) in the placebo group.513
In the MONARCH 2 phase III trial, patients who had disease progression
while receiving endocrine therapy were randomly assigned to fulvestrant
with or without abemaciclib.514 Those receiving combination therapy
experienced an improved PFS relative to those receiving fulvestrant
alone (16.4 vs 9.3 months; HR, 0.55; 95% CI, 0.45–0.68). The ORR was
higher in those receiving abemaciclib and fulvestrant (48% vs 21%).514 In
addition, an improvement was seen in OS with abemaciclib + fulvestrant
compared with fulvestrant alone (46.7 vs 37.3 months; HR, 0.757; 95%
CI, 0.606–0.945).515
The PALOMA-3 compared the combination of palbociclib and fulvestrant
to fulvestrant in pre- or postmenopausal patients with HR-positive,
HER2-negative advanced breast cancer, whose disease progressed on
prior endocrine therapy. Pre- or perimenopausal patients also received
goserelin. The median PFS was 9.5 months for the combination
compared to 4.6 months for fulvestrant (HR, 0.46; P < .000001).516 Grade
3/4 adverse events of palbociclib and fulvestrant were mainly confined to
neutropenia (in 65% of patients). The OS effect of palbociclib was
analyzed based on sensitivity to prior endocrine therapy, presence or
absence of visceral metastatic disease, and menopausal status; the
efficacy of subsequent therapies after disease progression; and safety.
Among patients (n = 410) with sensitivity to previous endocrine therapy
and no prior chemotherapy, the median OS was 39.7 months (95% CI,
34.8–45.7) in the palbociclib + fulvestrant group and 29.7 months (95%
CI, 23.8–37.9) in the placebo + fulvestrant group. However, it is
noteworthy that the differences in OS in the entire trial group were not
significant. Patients with visceral disease or those who had received
extensive prior chemotherapy had no significant improvements. Previous
chemotherapy had been administered to 34% of the trial patients. For
patients who had received previous chemotherapy, the median OS was
similar (25.6 vs. 26.2 months; HR, 0.91; 95% CI, 0.63-1.32). These
results suggest that palbociclib + fulvestrant combination improves OS in
patients who had not received previous chemotherapy for advanced
breast cancer and have endocrine therapy sensitivity.
Based on the above data that shows addition of a CDK4/6 inhibitor to
fulvestrant in patients previously exposed to prior endocrine therapy
provides a significant improvement in median PFS, the NCCN Panel has
included fulvestrant in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor as a preferred
option for postmenopausal and premenopausal patients with ovarian
ablation/suppression with HR-positive, HER2-negative, recurrent/stage
IV breast cancer who had disease progression on adjuvant endocrine
therapy or relapse within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy
completion. The eligibility criteria for MONALEESA-3, MONARCH 2 and
PALOMA-3 were different and likely impacted the final results. Since
ribociclib and abemaciclib in combination with fulvestrant have shown
OS benefit (in MONARCH 2 and MONALEESA-3 trials), these two
regimens are listed as category 1 options. The Panel notes that if there is
disease progression while on CDK4/6 inhibitor therapy, there are limited
data to support an additional line of therapy with another CDK4/6
inhibitor-containing regimen.
Preferred Second and/or Subsequent-Line Therapy for HR-Positive,
HER2-Negative Breast Cancer
Fulvestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor: The Panel recommends treatment with
Fulvestrant + CDK4/6 inhibitor abemaciclib, palbociclib, or ribociclib), if
CDK4/6 inhibitor was not previously used (category 1). The supporting
data for this recommendation comes from phase 3 randomized controlled
trials (MONARCH 2, PALOMA-3, and MONALEESA-3 discussed above)

# MS-65

<!-- ===== Page 192 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-65
of fulvestrant in combination with a CDK4/6 inhibitor (abemaciclib,
palbociclib, and ribociclib) showing OS benefit in the second-line setting,
especially in those who did not receive a previous CDK4/6 inhibitor.
If there is disease progression while on CDK4/6 inhibitor, there are
limited data to support the use of another CDK4/6 inhibitor + fulvestrant
in the second-line setting. The postMONARCH trial investigated a switch
in endocrine with/without CDK4/6 inhibition with abemaciclib after
disease progression on previous CDK4/6 inhibitor + AI as initial therapy
for advanced disease or recurrence on/after adjuvant CDK4/6i +
endocrine therapy.517 The majority of the patients in this trial (59%)
received palbociclib as their prior CDK 4/6 inhibitor therapy. This study
randomized patients (n = 368) to receive abemaciclib with fulvestrant or
placebo with fulvestrant. At the primary analysis (258 events), the HR
was 0.73 (95% CI, 0.57 to 0.95; nominal P = .017),  the median PFS was
6.0 (95% CI, 5.6 to 8.6) versus 5.3 (95% CI, 3.7 to 5.6).  PFS rate at 6
months was 50% with abemaciclib and fulvestrant versus 37% with
fulvestrant + placebo; this PFS benefit was supported by a blinded
independent review as well (HR, 0.55; 95% CI, 0.39–0.77) regardless of
ESR1 or PIK3CA status.517
Another phase II, double-blind placebo-controlled trial (MAINTAIN)
randomized patients whose cancer progressed on prior endocrine
therapy and CDK4/6i, to another endocrine therapy (fulvestrant or
exemestane) + ribociclib. Most patients on this trial previously received
palbociclib.518 The median PFS was 5.29 months with switching to
ribociclib + endocrine therapy versus 2.8 months after switching to
endocrine therapy + placebo (HR, 0.57; 95% CI, 0.39–0.85).518.
postMONARCH and MAINTAIN have not shown a OS benefit.
HR-positive breast cancers have been noted to harbor mutations in
components of the PI3K pathway. For patients with alterations in
PIK3CA/AKT1/PTEN who have disease progression on at least one
endocrine-based regimen in the metastatic setting or recurrence on
adjuvant endocrine therapy + CDK4/6 inhibitor, the panel recommends
fulvestrant in combination with either alpelisib or capivasertib.
Fulvestrant + Alpelisib: In a randomized phase III trial, patients (n = 572)
with advanced HR-positive breast cancer and confirmed PIK3CA status
had received a prior AI either for local or advanced disease. Patients
were enrolled into either the PIK3CA mutant (n = 341) cohort or the
PIK3CA non-mutant cohort and each cohort was randomized to receive
fulvestrant + alpelisib versus fulvestrant + placebo. Patients with a
PIK3CA mutation receiving alpelisib showed improved PFS compared to
fulvestrant alone. At a median follow-up of 20 months PFS was 11.0
months (95% CI, 7.5–14.5) in the alpelisib group compared with 5.7
months (95% CI, 3.7–7.4) in the group that received fulvestrant alone
(HR for progression or death, 0.65; 95% CI, 0.50–0.85; P < .001); in the
cohort without PIK3CA-mutated tumors, the HR was 0.85 (95% CI, 0.58–
1.25). In the overall population, the most frequently reported grade 3 or 4
adverse events seen with alpelisib and fulvestrant versus fulvestrant
alone were hyperglycemia (36.6% vs 0.7%), rash (9.9% vs 0.3%), and
diarrhea (6.7% vs 0.3%) (grade 3); no diarrhea or rash of grade 4 were
reported.519
Fulvestrant + Capivasertib: In the phase II FAKTION study, patients with
PI3K pathway-altered tumors (n = 76), treatment with fulvestrant +
capivasertib improved median PFS was 12.8 months compared with 4.6
months with fulvestrant + placebo. Median OS for PI3K pathway-altered
subgroup receiving capivasertib was 38.9 months compared with 20.0
months for those receiving placebo. No statistically significant differences
in either PFS or OS was seen in those with no PI3K pathway alterations
treated with capivasertib (n = 30) versus placebo (n = 34).
The randomized CAPItello-291, phase III trial has shown PFS benefits
with fulvestrant + capivasertib in patients (n = 708) with disease

# MS-66

<!-- ===== Page 193 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-66
progression on a previous AI, with or without a CDK4/6 inhibitor.520 The
median PFS in the entire study population was 7.2 months in the
fulvestrant + capivasertib group versus 3.6 months in the fulvestrant +
placebo group (HR, 0.60; 95% CI, 0.51–0.71). About 41% of patients (n
= 289) on the trial had PI3K pathway alterations.520 Among patients with
alterations in PI3K pathway-altered (PIK3CA or AKT1 activating
mutations or PTEN inactivating mutations and genomic changes [eg,
deletion]) the benefits were similar (PFS, 7.3 vs 3.1 months; HR, 0.50;
95% CI, 0.38–0.65).520 Although the PFS benefit was observed across all
subgroups, an exploratory analysis of patients with no alternations in the
PI3K pathway demonstrated a numerical but not statistically significant
increase in PFS (HR, 0.79; 95% CI, 0.61, 1.02) with capivasertib +
fulvestrant. 520
While the phase II FAKTION study demonstrated the benefit of adding
capivasertib being limited to tumors with AKT1/PTEN/PIK3CA mutations,
the phase III CAPItello-291 trial results showed benefit of fulvestrant +
capivasertib regardless of PI3K pathway alterations.
The estimated OS at 18 months was 74% with capivasertib-fulvestrant
and 65% with placebo-fulvestrant in the overall population (HR, 0.74;
95% CI, 0.56–0.98); and 73% versus 63%, respectively, in the PI3K
pathway-altered population (HR, 0.69; 95% CI, 0.45–1.05). The most
frequent adverse events of grade ≥3 in patients receiving fulvestrant +
capivasertib were rash (in 12.1% of patients, vs in 0.3% of those
receiving placebo-fulvestrant) and diarrhea (in 9.3% vs 0.3%). Any grade
hyperglycemia was seen in 16.3% of patients. Fulvestrant + capivasertib
has been U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved for
patients whose tumors harbor alterations in PIK3CA, AKT1, or PTEN.
Fulvestrant/Gedatolisib ± Palbociclib:
Everolimus + endocrine therapy (exemestane, fulvestrant, tamoxifen):
Resistance to endocrine therapy in patients with HR-positive disease is
frequent. One mechanism of resistance to endocrine therapy is activation
of the mammalian target of rapamycin (mTOR) signal transduction
pathway.
A randomized phase II study estimated the efficacy of tamoxifen alone
versus tamoxifen combined with everolimus, an oral inhibitor of mTOR,
in patients with HR-positive, HER2-negative metastatic breast cancer
previously treated with an AI.521 After a median follow-up of 13 months,
an intent-to-treat analysis showed that the clinical benefit was 42.1%
(95% CI, 29.1–55.9) with tamoxifen alone and 61.1% (95% CI, 46.9–
74.1) with tamoxifen + everolimus. An improvement in median time to
progression was seen when everolimus was combined with tamoxifen
compared with tamoxifen alone. Median time to progression was 4.5
months (95% CI, 3.7–8.7) with tamoxifen alone versus 8.5 months (95%
CI, 6.01–13.9) with everolimus and tamoxifen.521
In a phase III trial in postmenopausal patients with advanced,
HR-positive breast cancer with no prior endocrine therapy for advanced
disease, randomized subjects to letrozole with or without the mTOR
inhibitor temsirolimus,522 PFS was not different between the treatment
arms (HR, 0.89; 95% CI, 0.75–1.05; log-rank P = .18).
The results of this trial differ from that of the BOLERO-2 trial (described
below). The reasons for the differences in the outcomes of these two
randomized phase III studies522,523 is uncertain, but may be related to the
issues of patient selection and extent of prior endocrine therapy.
A phase III study (BOLERO-2) randomized postmenopausal patients with
HR-positive advanced breast cancer that had disease progression or

# MS-67

<!-- ===== Page 194 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-67
recurrence during treatment with a nonsteroidal AI to exemestane with or
without the mTOR inhibitor everolimus.524 Final results reported after
median 18-month follow-up show that median PFS (by central review)
remained significantly longer with everolimus + exemestane versus
placebo + exemestane at 11.0 versus 4.1 months, respectively (HR,
0.38; 95% CI, 0.31–0.48; P < .0001).523 The adverse events (all grades)
that occurred more frequently in those receiving everolimus included
stomatitis, infections, rash, pneumonitis, and hyperglycemia.523,524
Analysis of safety and efficacy in the older patients enrolled in this trial
showed that older patients treated with an everolimus-containing
regimen had similar incidences of these adverse events, but the younger
patients had more on-treatment deaths.525 Based on the evidence from
the BOLERO-2 trial, the NCCN Panel has included everolimus +
exemestane as an option for patients who fulfill the entry criteria for
BOLERO-2. Tamoxifen or fulvestrant in combination with everolimus
have also been included as options. The NCCN Panel also notes that if
there is disease progression while on an everolimus-containing regimen,
there are no data to support an additional line of therapy with another
everolimus regimen.
Other Recommended Regimens in First and/or Subsequent Lines
ESR1 activating mutations are frequently detected in patients with prior
exposure to AIs.526,527 After a prior line of AI with or without CDK4/6
inhibitor therapy (in adjuvant or metastatic setting), options for ESR-1
mutated tumors include elacestrant or imlunestrant.
Elacestrant: In the phase III EMERALD trial, elacestrant was compared
with standard endocrine therapy (fulvestrant or aromatase inhibitor) in
patients (n = 477) with ER-positive, HER2-negative metastatic breast
cancer who had received at least one prior line of endocrine therapy and
whose disease had progressed on a CDK4/6 inhibitor. Of the study
population, 72% had ESR-1 mutated tumors. In overall study population,
elacestrant showed a median PFS of 2.79 months compared to 1.9
months with standard of care (including fulvestrant) - a 30% reduction in
the risk of progression or death with the elacestrant (HR, 0.69; P
=.0018).528 In a subgroup analysis of patients with prior endocrine
therapy + CDK4/6 inhibitor ≥12 months, the median PFS with elacestrant
was 8.6 versus 1.9 months with SOC (HR, 0.41; 95% CI, 0.26–0.63). 529
The most common adverse events with elacestrant versus standard
endocrine monotherapy included nausea (35% vs 19%), vomiting (19%
vs 9%), decreased appetite (15% vs 9.2%), and arthralgia (14% vs16%).
Elacestrant was effective even in patients who previously progressed
on fulvestrant, due to higher bioavailability and potency. Therefore,
NCCN Panel recommends elacestrant as an option after a prior line of AI
a with CDK4/6 inhibitor therapy (in adjuvant or metastatic setting).
Imlunestrant: The phase III EMBER-3 trial randomized patients (n = 874)
with ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced breast cancer whose disease
had progressed on AI therapy, with or without a CDK4/6 inhibitor to
receive imlunestrant or standard endocrine therapy (exemestane or
fulvestrant) or combination of imlunestrant and abemaciclib.530
Single agent imlunestrant improved median PFS compared with standard
therapy (5.5 vs 3.8 months, HR 0.62 (0.44-0.82), P< .001), among
patients with ESR1 mutations; this PFS benefit was not observed in the
overall population.530 The most frequently reported adverse events with
imlunestrant versus standard endocrine therapy were fatigue (23% vs
13%), diarrhea (21% vs12%) and nausea (17% vs 13%).530 The NCCN
Panel recommends imlunestrant after a prior line of AI with or without
CDK4/6 inhibitor therapy (in adjuvant or metastatic setting).
The combination of imlunestrant to abemaciclib showed improved PFS in
patients with both ESR1-mutated and wild-type cancers (9.4 vs 5.5
months; HR, 0.57; 95% CI, 0.44–0.73). Those with ESR1-mutated
disease who received imlunestrant + abemaciclib (n = 67) experienced a

# MS-68

<!-- ===== Page 195 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-68
greater PFS benefit with a median PFS of 11.1 months (95% CI, 7.4–
13.7) versus 5.5 months with imlunestrant alone (n = 92; HR, 0.53; 95%
CI, 0.35–0.80). In those previously treated with a CDK4/6 inhibitor with
ESR1-mutated tumors, the median PFS with doublet (n = 53) versus the
single agent (n = 72) was 11.1 versus 5.4 months, respectively (HR,
0.44; 95% CI, 0.28–0.70). The grade ≥3 toxicities were higher with the
combination therapy, seen in 49% versus 17%. While combination
showed a PFS benefit in the overall population, it is noteworthy that the
trial design did not include a comparison with the established
combination of fulvestrant + abemaciclib. Therefore, the panel has the
doublet as useful in certain circumstances noting that the combination of
imlunestrant and abemaciclib may be most effective in those with ESR1
mutations and no prior CDK4/6 inhibitor.530
Vepdegestrant: The phase 3 VERITAC-2 trial showed that the PROTAC
degrader, vepdegestrant significantly extended median PFS to 5.0
months (95% CI, 3.7 to 7.4) compared to 2.1 months (95% CI, 1.9 to 3.5)
with fulvestrant in patients with ESR1-mutated HR-positive/HER2 breast
cancer (HR 0.58; [95% CI, 0.43 to 0.78]; P < .001 ).The study did not
meet its primary endpoint in the overall population. In the overall
population, the median PFS was 3.8 months (95% CI, 3.7 to 5.3) with
vepdegestrant and 3.6 months (95% CI, 2.6 to 4.0) with fulvestrant (HR,
0.83 [95% CI, 0.69 to 1.01]; P= .07). Adverse events of grade 3 or higher
occurred in 23.4% of the patients treated with vepdegestrant and in
17.6% of the patients treated with fulvestrant. Adverse events leading to
treatment discontinuation were seen in 2.9% and 0.7% of the patients
treated with vepdegestrant versus fulvestrant, respectively.  The most
common associated with vepdegestrant were fatigue, nausea and
elevated liver enzymes, grade1 or 2. NCCN panel has included
vepdegestrant as an option after a prior line of prior endocrine therapy in
the metastatic setting for patients with ESR1-mutated breast cancer.
Fulvestrant + nonsteroidal AI: The combination of two endocrine agents
as first- and subsequent- line treatment in postmenopausal patients with
HR-positive, metastatic breast cancer has been reported from studies
comparing single-agent anastrozole versus anastrozole + fulvestrant.
In one study (FACT), the combination of fulvestrant with anastrozole was
not superior to single-agent anastrozole (time to progression HR, 0.99;
95% CI, 0.81–1.20; P = .91).531 In a second phase III trial (SoFEA), the
effect of fulvestrant alone or in combination with anastrozole or
exemestane was studied in patients with advanced breast cancer with
acquired resistance to a nonsteroidal AI.532 An AI had been given as
adjuvant treatment to 18% of patients for a median of 27.9 months, and
to 82% of patients for locally advanced/metastatic disease for a median
of 19.3 months. Median PFS was 4.8 months, 4.4 months, and 3.4
months for patients treated with fulvestrant alone, anastrozole +
fulvestrant, and fulvestrant + exemestane, respectively. No differences
were observed for ORR, clinical benefit rate, and OS.
In the trial by the Southwest Oncology Group (SWOG), S0226, PFS (HR,
0.80; 95% CI, 0.68–0.94; stratified log-rank P = .007) and OS (HR, 0.81;
95% CI, 0.65–1.00; stratified P = .049) were superior with combination
anastrozole + fulvestrant.533 A subgroup analysis in this trial suggested
that patients without prior adjuvant tamoxifen experienced the greatest
OS benefit with combination therapy compared with monotherapy
(median, 52.2 months vs 40.3 months, respectively; HR, 0.73; 95% CI,
0.58–0.92).534
The reasons for the divergent outcomes in the FACT, SoFEA, and
SWOG trials are not clear. The three trials discussed above had slightly
different patient populations—there were more cases of patients with no
prior endocrine exposure (with de novo stage IV metastatic disease) in
the SWOG S0226 trial compared with the FACT trial. The FACT trial
included a more heterogeneous population of both premenopausal

# MS-69

<!-- ===== Page 196 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-69
(treated with GNRH agonist for ovarian function suppression [OFS]) and
postmenopausal patients with heterogeneous disease setting (locally
advanced and metastatic disease, and more prior endocrine exposure)
compared with SWOG S0226. The SoFEA trial enrolled patients with
acquired endocrine resistance (who had disease progression while they
were receiving an AI). Further studies are needed to confirm the results
of the SWOG S0226 trial. However, in the current era, combining
endocrine with targeted therapy is the standard, therefore the role of
combination endocrine therapy is limited.
Monotherapy with Endocrine Agents:
Aromatase inhibitors (AIs): AIs as monotherapy are options as
subsequent-line therapy. The three AIs (anastrozole, letrozole, and
exemestane) have shown similar efficacy in the second-line setting.535-537
AI monotherapy maybe be useful in patients desiring single-agent
treatment, if they have not received an AI as first-line treatment or if they
may not be suitable for combination therapy. Patients who have received
a prior nonsteroidal AI may benefit from a steroidal AI as a subsequent
line of therapy or vice versa.
Selective ER modulator (SERM): Tamoxifen is the commonly used
SERM for premenopausal patients.538 In postmenopausal patients, AI
monotherapy has been shown to have superior outcome compared with
tamoxifen, although the differences are modest.539-543 A randomized
phase III trial comparing tamoxifen with exemestane as first-line
endocrine therapy for postmenopausal patients with metastatic breast
cancer showed no significant differences in PFS or OS between the two
arms.541
An analysis of two randomized studies of first-line treatment with
anastrozole followed by second-line tamoxifen and vice versa showed
that tamoxifen is effective as a second-line option.544
Fulvestrant: Results from phase III trial (FALCON) of first-line treatment
with single agent fulvestrant compared with anastrozole in endocrine
therapy-naïve patients with metastatic ER-positive breast cancer,
demonstrated improved PFS with fulvestrant (at the higher dose, 500
mg) over anastrozole at a median follow-up of 25.0 months (16.6 vs 13.8
months; HR, 0.797; 95% CI, 0.637–0.999).545 The QOL outcomes were
similar between the two groups, with the most common adverse effects
for fulvestrant and anastrozole, respectively were arthralgia (17% vs
10%) and hot flashes (11% vs 10%).545
Fulvestrant appears to be as effective as anastrozole in terms of
response in patients whose disease progressed on previous
tamoxifen.546,547 A randomized phase II study compared anastrozole
versus fulvestrant in over 200 patients with advanced breast
cancer.548,549 In the initial analysis, fulvestrant was as effective as
anastrozole in terms of ORR (36.0% vs 35.5%; OR, 1.02; 95% CI, 0.56–
1.87; P = .947) in evaluable patients (n = 89 for fulvestrant and n = 93 for
anastrozole).548 An improved time to progression was seen with
fulvestrant compared to anastrozole (median time to progression was
23.4 months for fulvestrant vs 13.1 months for anastrozole; HR, 0.63;
95% CI, 0.39–1.00; P = .0496).549 The median OS was observed to be
longer in the fulvestrant group than in the anastrozole group (54.1 vs
48.4 months; HR, 0.70; P = .041).550
The clinical benefit rates of fulvestrant versus exemestane were
observed in a phase III trial of postmenopausal patients with HR-positive
advanced breast cancer who experienced disease progression on prior
nonsteroidal AI therapy were comparable (32.2% vs 31.5%; P = .853).551
A phase III randomized study in postmenopausal patients with metastatic
HR-positive breast cancer compared fulvestrant 500 mg every 2 weeks
for 3 doses followed by 500 mg monthly versus fulvestrant 250 mg
monthly. The PFS was superior with the fulvestrant 500 mg regimen (HR,

# MS-70

<!-- ===== Page 197 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-70
0.80; 95% CI, 0.68–0.94; P = .006),552 indicating an increased duration of
response (DOR) with the higher dose of fulvestrant. The final analyses
demonstrated an increase in median OS (4.1 months) and reduced risk
of death (19%) with a dose of 500 mg compared with 250 mg. Median
OS was 26.4 versus 22.3 months (HR, 0.81; 95% CI, 0.69–0.96; P =
.02).553
Regimens Useful in Certain Circumstances for Therapy for
Second/Subsequent-Line Therapy of HR-Positive, HER2-Negative
Breast Cancer
Megestrol acetate,554-557 estradiol558 androgens such as fluoxymesterone,
single-agent abemaciclib the combination of imlunestrant of abemaciclib,
and biomarker-directed targeted therapies (discussed in section:
Additional Targeted Therapies) have been listed as options useful in
certain circumstances.
The phase II MONARCH 1 trial evaluated the activity of abemaciclib as a
single agent in patients (n = 132) with refractory HR-positive,
HER2-negative metastatic breast cancer who had disease progression
while on endocrine therapy and already received multiple systemic
therapies (average of three prior systemic regimens).559 Ninety percent of
patients had visceral disease and 50.8% had more than three sites of
metastases.559 Single-agent abemaciclib induced a partial response in 26
patients (19.7%) and demonstrated an ORR of 19.7% (95% CI, 13.3–
27.5).559 Median PFS was 6 months (95% CI, 4.2–7.5). At the final
analysis, at 18 months, median OS was 22.3 months (95% CI, 17.7–not
reached).559 Diarrhea was the most frequent adverse event reported in
90.2% of patients. Other common adverse events were fatigue (65.2%),
nausea (64.4%), and decreased appetite (45.5%). Grade 3 and 4
neutropenia occurred in 26.9% of patients.559 The NCCN Panel has
included single-agent abemaciclib as an option for those with disease
progression on prior endocrine therapy and prior chemotherapy in the
metastatic setting.
Based on the data from the EMBER-3 trial (discussed above) the Panel
has the doublet as useful in certain circumstances noting that the
combination of imlunestrant of abemaciclib may be most effective in
those with ESR1 mutations and no prior CDK4/6 inhibitor.530
Cytotoxic Therapy for HR-Positive, HER2-Negative Breast Cancer:
Endocrine Refractory or with Visceral Crisis
First-line regimens: For those with germline BRCA1/2 pathogenic variant
(PV), treatment with a PARP inhibitor (olaparib or talazoparib) can be
considered in the first-line (category 1, preferred option). The data for
this is discussed in section: Therapy for Germline BRCA1/2 Pathogenic
Variants. The NCCN Panel notes that while the available evidence
suggests that use of PARP inhibitors is more effective if used in earlier
lines of therapy, it may be considered for a later line as well.
For patients with no BRCA 1/2 mutations with HR-positive, HER2-
negative metastatic breast cancer that is refractory to endocrine therapy,
the first-line options include single-agent chemotherapy (category 1,
preferred), discussed under Cytotoxic therapy for HR-Positive, HER2-
Negative Breast Cancer: Endocrine Refractory or with Visceral Crisis, or
fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki (category 2A, other recommended
option), if the tumor is HER2 (ERBB2) IHC 0+,1+, or 2+/ISH negative.
In the DESTINY-Breast06 trial of patients (n = 866) with HR-positive,
HER2-low (HER2 (ERBB2) IHC 1+ or 2+/ISH negative) or HER2-ultralow
(HER2 [ERBB2) IHC 0+] metastatic breast cancer (previously treated
with at least one line of endocrine therapy, median two lines, and no prior
chemotherapy for metastatic disease), in the HER2-low subgroup (n =
713), fam-trastuzumab-nxki significantly improved PFS compared with
capecitabine or taxane chemotherapy (13.2 vs 8.1 months; HR, 0.62;
95% CI, 0.51–0.71).560 The OS differences were not yet statistically
significant. In the HER2-ultralow subgroup (n = 153), efficacy was similar
(PFS, 13.2 vs 8.3 months; HR, 0.78; 95% CI, 0.50–1.21).560 Grade ≥3

# MS-71

<!-- ===== Page 198 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-71
adverse events occurred in 53% of patients receiving fam-trastuzumab-
nxki and 44% receiving chemotherapy. Drug-related interstitial lung
disease (ILD) or pneumonitis occurred in 11% of patients in the fam-
trastuzumab-nxki group, including 0.7% with fatal (grade 5) events. Since
am-trastuzumab-nxki is associated with ILD/pneumonitis. The Panel
recommends regular monitoring for this serious side effect. For patients
with a history of ILD/pneumonitis, there are no data on managing safety
or toxicity of this drug in a trial. Based on the results of the DESTINY-
Breast06 trial, fam trastuzumab-nxki is included as a category 2A, other
recommended first-line option for those with HER2 (ERBB2) IHC 0+, 1+,
or 2+/ISH negative and HR -positive, endocrine-refractory tumors.
Notably, the DESTINY-Breast06 trial did not allow crossover, and given
the significant benefit of fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan in later lines
settings (DESTINY-Breast04 trial) this study does not inform whether
giving fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan in chemo-therapy naïve versus later
line settings is optimal. Thus, selection of chemotherapy versus fam-
trastuzumab-nxki for first-line therapy should be individualized based on
clinical features and the patient. Fam-trastuzumab-nxki may be an option
for those with more rapidly progressive or symptomatic disease in those
previously treated with at least one line of endocrine-based therapy in
the metastatic setting based on the results of the DESTINY-Breast06
trial.560
Second and subsequent line regimen options: As second- and
subsequent-line options, antibody-drug conjugates (fam-trastuzumab-
nxki, sacituzumab govitecan and datopotamab deruxtecan-dlnk) have
been shown to be effective in addition to the available chemotherapy
options.
The phase III DESTINY-Breast04 trial included patients with HER2-low
(HER2 [ERBB2] IHC 1+ or 2+/ISH negative) metastatic breast cancer
who had received one or two prior chemotherapy regimens. Patients (n =
557) were randomized (2:1) to fam-trastuzumab-nxki or physician’s
choice of chemotherapy. The median PFS was 9.9 months with fam-
trastuzumab-nxki versus 5.1 months with chemotherapy (HR, 0.50), and
median OS was 23 versus 17 months (HR, 0.69).
Among the ~90% of patients with HR-positive disease, median PFS was
10.1 versus 5.4 months (HR, 0.51; 95% CI, 0.40–0.64), and OS was 23.9
versus 17.6 months (HR, 0.69; 95% CI, 0.55–0.87). Grade ≥3 adverse
events occurred in 53% of the fam-trastuzumab-nxki group and 67% of
the chemotherapy group. ILD or pneumonitis occurred in 12% of patients
receiving fam-trastuzumab-nxki, with 0.8% fatal events. Based on the
results of the DESTINY-Breast04 trial, fam trastuzumab-nxki is included
as a category 1 preferred second-line option for those with HER2
(ERBB2) IHC 1+, or 2+/ISH negative and HR-positive, endocrine-
refractory tumors. Based on the results of the DESTINY-Breast04 trial,
the NCCN Panel has included fam-trastuzumab-nxki as a category 1,
preferred option for those with HER2-low (HER2 [ERBB2] IHC 1+ or
2+/ISH negative) metastatic breast cancer. For those with HER2
(ERBB2) IHC 0+, (which were not included in DB-04 trial but based on
the DESTINY-Breast06 trial which has not yet shown an OS benefit),
fam-trastuzumab-nxki is a category 2A, other recommended option.
If not a candidate for fam-trastuzumab-nxki, the options include
sacituzumab govitecan, chemotherapy (discussed in section: Cytotoxic
Therapy for HR-Positive, HER2-Negative Breast Cancer: Endocrine
Refractory or with Visceral Crisis), datopotamab deruxtecan-dlnk , or
targeted therapy (discussed in section: Additional Targeted Therapies)
The data for use of sacituzumab govitecan in this setting comes from the
TROPiCS-02 trial.561 Patients (n = 543) with HR+ and HER2–-negative
locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic breast cancer and had received
at least one previous endocrine therapy, a taxane, and a CDK4/6
inhibitor in any setting and two to four previous chemotherapy regimens

# MS-72

<!-- ===== Page 199 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-72
for metastatic disease were randomized to receive sacituzumab
govitecan or chemotherapy. With a median 12.5-month follow-up, the OS
was significantly improved with sacituzumab govitecan versus
chemotherapy (median, 14.4 vs 11.2 months; HR, 0.79; 95% CI, 0.65–
0.96; P = .020). The ORR was significantly improved with sacituzumab
govitecan compared with chemotherapy (57 [21%] patients vs 38
[14%]).561 Sacituzumab govitecan was associated with higher rates of
common treatment-emergent adverse events compared with
chemotherapy, particularly neutropenia (71% vs.55%), diarrhea (62% vs.
22%), nausea (59% vs. 35%), alopecia (48% vs. 18%), and anemia(37%
vs. 28%); severe (grade ≥3) neutropenia and diarrhea were also more
frequent.  Use of G-CSF  was higher with sacituzumab govitecan,
reflecting increased hematologic toxicity, although rates of febrile
neutropenia were similar between groups. Neuropathy and pneumonitis
were less frequent with sacituzumab govitecan, and treatment
discontinuations were uncommon in both groups. Based on the results of
the TROPiCS-02 trial, sacituzumab govitecan is included as a category
1, preferred option in the second-line setting.
The phase III, open-label, randomized TROPION-Breast01 study
compared datopotamab deruxtecan-dlnk with investigator's choice of
chemotherapy in patients with HR-positive/HER2-negative breast cancer
who had disease progression on endocrine therapy and had received
one or more previous lines of chemotherapy in the metastatic
setting.562,563
A PFS benefit was observed with datopotamab deruxtecan-dlnk in the
TROPION-Breast01. This PFS benefit was maintained over time, with 9-
month PFS rates approximately double with datopotamab deruxtecan-
dlnk compared with chemotherapy (37.5% vs 18.7%, HR, 0.63; P <
.0001), and 12-month PFS rates of 25.5% versus 14.6%, HR, 0.64 (95%
CI, 0.53 to 0.76). Datopotamab deruxtecan-dlnk, however,  the OS was
not statistically significant (HR 1.01, 95% CI 0.83-1.22, P = .9445).563
The previously approved antibody-drug conjugates (ADCs), fam-
trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki and sacituzumab govitecan-hziy, have
shown a benefit in OS in randomized phase III trials (DESTINY-Breast06
and TROPiCS-02 trials). In addition, the benefit of using datopotamab
deruxtecan-dlnk (or any ADC) in patients with prior ADC treatment is not
known, as the TROPION-Breast01 trial did not include patients with prior
ADC treatment. Trials are underway to address sequencing.
Datopotamab deruxtecan-dlnk is currently included as category 2A, other
recommended option.
Third-line and beyond: The third-line and beyond, the Panel
recommends systemic chemotherapy or targeted agents as appropriate
(discussed in the section titled: Additional Targeted Therapies).
Therapy for HER2-Positive Breast Cancer
For patients with HER2-positive, recurrent/stage IV breast cancer, the
treatment approach is HER2-targeted therapy in combination with cytoxic
therapy (which includes chemotherapy or ADCs, or other targeted
therapy). Prior exposure to trastuzumab during adjuvant treatment can
reduce the effectiveness of first-line trastuzumab-based therapy.
Patients with disease progression while being treated with
HER2-targeted therapy should be offered an additional line of treatment
with a HER2-targeted therapy since it is beneficial to continue
suppression of the HER2 pathway. The choice of the HER2-targeted
therapy will depend on previously administered therapy, relapse-free
interval, and patients’ preference and access.
The optimal sequence of available HER2-targeted therapies and the
optimal duration of HER2-targeted therapy for recurrent/stage IV breast
cancer is currently unknown. The NCCN Panel recommends continuing
HER2-targted therapy until progression/unacceptable toxicity.

# MS-73

<!-- ===== Page 200 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-73
Preferred First-line Regimens for HR-Positive or Negative,
HER2-Positive Breast Cancer
Pertuzumab + trastuzumab in combination with a taxane (THP) followed
by maintenance pertuzumab + trastuzumab: A randomized, double-blind,
phase III study (CLEOPATRA) compared the efficacy and safety of
pertuzumab in combination with trastuzumab and docetaxel versus
trastuzumab and docetaxel as first-line treatment for 808 patients (n =
808) with HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer.564 This trial included
patients (about 10%) who had previously received trastuzumab in the
adjuvant or neoadjuvant setting. At a median follow-up of 19 months, the
addition of pertuzumab to docetaxel + trastuzumab resulted in
improvement in PFS compared with placebo (median, 18.5 vs 12.4
months; HR, 0.62; 95% CI, 0.51–0.75; P < .001).564 At a median
follow-up of 30 months, the results showed a statistically significant
improvement in OS in favor of the pertuzumab-containing regimen, with
a 34% reduction in the risk of death (HR, 0.66; 95% CI, 0.52–0.84; P =
.0008).565 The most common adverse reactions reported in the
pertuzumab group compared to the control group were diarrhea (67% vs
46%), rash (34% vs 24%), mucosal inflammation (27% vs 20%), febrile
neutropenia (14% vs 8%), and dry skin (10% vs 4%). Peripheral edema
and constipation were greater in the control group.564 Cardiac adverse
events or left ventricular systolic dysfunction were reported slightly more
frequently in the control group.566 HRQOL was not different in the two
treatment groups.567 In the PERUSE study, patients (n = 1436) with
advanced HER2-positive breast cancer and no prior systemic therapy
(except endocrine therapy) received docetaxel, paclitaxel, or
nab-paclitaxel with trastuzumab and pertuzumab until disease
progression or unacceptable toxicity. The preliminary results after 52
months of median follow-up show that median PFS was comparable
between docetaxel, paclitaxel, and nab-paclitaxel (median PFS reported
was 19.6, 23.0, and 18.1 months with docetaxel, paclitaxel, and
nab-paclitaxel, respectively).568 Compared with docetaxel-containing
therapy, paclitaxel-containing therapy was associated with more
neuropathy (31% vs 16%), but less febrile neutropenia (1% vs 11%) and
mucositis (14% vs 25%). Phase II trials have also found activity and
tolerability for pertuzumab, pertuzumab with trastuzumab, and for other
regimens combining pertuzumab and trastuzumab together with other
active cytotoxic agents (ie, paclitaxel, vinorelbine).569,570 Phase III trials of
pertuzumab + chemotherapy without trastuzumab have not been
reported.
After treatment with taxane -based chemotherapy in combination with
pertuzumab + trastuzumab, based on the results of the PATINA trial571
(discussed in section below), maintenance therapy with trastuzumab +/-
pertuzumab may be offered for those with HR-negative, HER2-postive
disease with the addition of endocrine therapy (AI ± palbociclib) if
patients have HR-positive, HER2-positive disease.
The NCCN Panel recommends pertuzumab + trastuzumab in
combination with a taxane (THP) followed by maintenance pertuzumab +
trastuzumab as a preferred option for first-line treatment of patients with
HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer. Pertuzumab + trastuzumab in
combination with docetaxel is an NCCN category 1 recommendation
(based on data from CLEOPATRA trial) and in combination with
paclitaxel is an NCCN category 2A recommendation.
Other Recommended First- line Regimen for HR-Positive or Negative,
HER2-Positive Breast Cancer
Fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki + Pertuzumab: In the first-line setting,
the combination of fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan nxki and pertuzumab
has demonstrated improved outcomes compared with the regimen of a
taxane, trastuzumab, and pertuzumab (THP). This regimen may be
considered a reasonable alternative to THP, especially in individuals with
central nervous system (CNS) involvement. However, due to the risk of

# MS-74

<!-- ===== Page 201 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-74
interstitial pneumonitis, it is generally not the preferred first-line option for
most patients presenting with de novo metastatic disease.
Evidence supporting this approach comes from a randomized trial
(DESTINY-Breast09) of patients (n = 770) with metastatic HER2-positive
breast cancer.572 Median PFS was longer with fam-trastuzumab
deruxtecan + pertuzumab (40.7 months) compared with THP (26.9
months), corresponding to a HR of 0.46 (95% CI, 0.44–0.71).572
Response rates were 85% versus 79%, respectively, and the median
DOR was 39 months compared with 26 months. Although participants
had not received prior therapy for metastatic disease, about 44% had
undergone earlier neoadjuvant or adjuvant treatment.572 The frequency
of grade ≥3 adverse events was comparable between the two groups
(64% vs 62%). With fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan + pertuzumab, the
most common severe toxicities included neutropenia, hypokalemia, and
anemia, whereas neutropenia, leukopenia, and diarrhea were more
common with THP. Drug-related interstitial lung disease or pneumonitis
occurred more frequently with fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan +
pertuzumab (12%), including rare fatal cases, compared with 1% in the
THP group. Fatal adverse events (grade 5) were also more with fam-
trastuzumab deruxtecan + pertuzumab (3.4% vs 0.8%).
A major limitation of the DESTINY-Breast09 study is its lack of
crossover. Fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan is known to be active in later
line settings (DESTINY-Breast02/03) and by not allowing crossover very
few patients received subsequent fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan, thus not
informing whether front line administration is superior to later line
administration. This is particularly relevant in this setting where patients
have less toxic options.
Therefore, according to the NCCN Panel, for most patients with
previously untreated HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer, THP
remains the preferred approach, fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki +
pertuzumab is included as other recommended option. Considering that
fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki is associated with ILD/pneumonitis,
the Panel recommends regular monitoring for this serious side effect is
recommended.
Preferred Second/Third-line Regimens for HR-Positive or Negative,
HER2-Positive Breast Cancer
Capecitabine/Tucatinib + Trastuzumab: In a randomized study of heavily
pretreated patients (n = 480 ) with HER2-positive metastatic breast
cancer (median of four prior treatment lines), the addition of tucatinib to
capecitabine and trastuzumab significantly improved outcomes. The 1-
year PFS rate was 33% with tucatinib versus 12% without (HR for
progression or death 0.54; 95% CI, 0.42–0.71), with median PFS of 7.8
versus 5.6 months, respectively. OS at 2 years was 45% in the tucatinib
group compared with 27% in the control group (HR for death 0.66; 95%
CI, 0.50–0.88). Among patients with brain metastases, the 1-year PFS
rate was 25% with tucatinib versus 0% without. The most common grade
≥3 adverse events in the tucatinib combination group, compared with the
placebo combination group, included palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia
(13% vs 9%), diarrhea (13% vs 9%), elevated ALT, and AST levels
(approximately 5% vs 0.5% for each), and fatigue (5% vs 4%).
The NCCN Panel has added this regimen as a category 1, preferred
option in patients with both systemic and CNS progression in the third-
line setting and beyond; and note that it may be given in the second-line
setting.
Fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan-nxki: In the randomized DESTINY-
Breast02 study of patients previously treated with T-DM1, fam-
trastuzumab deruxtecan nxki demonstrated superior efficacy compared
with physician’s choice therapy (capecitabine plus either trastuzumab or
lapatinib).573 Median PFS was 17.8 months versus 6.9 months (HR 0.36,
95% CI 0.28–0.45), and median OS was 39.2 months versus 26.5

# MS-75

<!-- ===== Page 202 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-75
months (HR 0.66, 95% CI 0.50–0.86). Patient-reported outcomes also
favored fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan.573
In the phase III DESTINY-Breast03 trial of patients (n = 524) with
disease progression after trastuzumab- and taxane-based therapy,
median PFS was 29 months with fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan compared
with 7.2 months for T-DM1 (HR, 0.30; 95% CI, 0.24–0.38).574 OS was
also longer with fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan (median 52.6 vs 42.7
months; HR, 0.73; 95% CI, 0.56–0.94).574 Grade ≥3 adverse events were
reported in 56% of patients receiving fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan and
52% of those receiving T-DM1. Interstitial lung disease occurred more
frequently with fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan (16.7% vs 3.4%).This study
also did not allow crossover and given the known activity of later line
fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan (DESTINY-Breast02) it does not directly
inform whether second line administration is superior to later lines.
In the randomized DESTINY-Breast02 study of patients previously
treated with T-DM1, fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan nxki demonstrated
superior efficacy compared with physician’s choice therapy (capecitabine
+ either trastuzumab or lapatinib).573 Median PFS was 17.8 versus 6.9
months (HR, 0.36; 95% CI, 0.28–0.45), and median OS was 39.2 versus
26.5 months (HR, 0.66; 95% CI, 0.50–0.86). Patient-reported outcomes
also favored fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan.573
According to the NCCN Panel, fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan nxki as a
single agent is a recommended option to consider in the second-line
setting following disease progression on THP. It may be considered for
select individuals whose disease recurs within 6 months of completing
neoadjuvant/adjuvant therapy or within 12 months after treatment
pertuzumab-containing regimens.
Other Recommended Third-line Regimens for HR-Positive or Negative,
HER2-Positive Breast Cancer
Ado-trastuzumab emtansine (T-DM1): T-DM1 is an antibody-drug
conjugate that stably links the HER2-targeting property of trastuzumab to
the cytotoxic activity of the microtubule-inhibitory agent DM1 (derivative
of maytansine).
In a phase III trial (MARIANNE), 1095 patients with locally advanced or
metastatic breast cancer were randomized to first-line treatment with
T-DM1 with or without pertuzumab or trastuzumab + a taxane. The
primary endpoints were safety and PFS assessed by independent
review. The PFS for T-DM1 with pertuzumab was found to be noninferior
to trastuzumab and a taxane (15.2 and 13.7 months, respectively; HR,
0.87; 97.5% CI, 0.69–1.08; P = .14).575 The PFS for T-DM1 alone was
noninferior to trastuzumab + a taxane (14.1 and 13.7, respectively; HR,
0.91; 97.5% CI, 0.73–1.13; P = .31).575 The incidence of grade 3–5
adverse events was 54.1%, 45.4%, and 46.2% in the trastuzumab + a
taxane arm, T-DM1 arm, and T-DM1 + pertuzumab arm, respectively.
HRQOL was maintained for a longer duration with a median of 7.7
months for T-DM1 (HR, 0.70; 95% CI, 0.57–0.86) and a median of 9
months for T-DM1 + pertuzumab (HR, 0.68; 95% CI, 0.55–0.84)
compared with a median of 3.9 months for trastuzumab and a taxane.575
Based on the MARIANNE trial data demonstrating T-DM1 and T-DM1
with pertuzumab being noninferior, with better QOL compared with
trastuzumab + a taxane and possibly better tolerated for some
patients,575 the NCCN Panel included T-DM1 as an option for treatment
of patients with HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer. Pertuzumab,
trastuzumab, and a taxane, however, remains the preferred first-line
regimen for HER2-positive metastatic disease based on data
demonstrating improved OS compared to trastuzumab and a taxane.

# MS-76

<!-- ===== Page 203 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-76
TDM-1 as first-line therapy should be considered only in those not
suitable for the preferred treatment.
T-DM1 also has also shown activity in the second-line setting. A
randomized, international, multicenter, open-label, phase III study
(EMILIA) evaluated the safety and efficacy of T-DM1 compared with
lapatinib + capecitabine for HER2-positive patients with locally advanced
breast cancer or metastatic breast cancer previously treated with
trastuzumab and a taxane.576 The primary endpoints of this study were
PFS, OS, and safety. T-DM1 demonstrated a statistically significant
improvement in both primary endpoints of PFS and OS. PFS (assessed
by independent review) was significantly improved with T-DM1 with
median PFS of 9.6 months versus 6.4 months with lapatinib +
capecitabine; HR for progression or death from any cause was 0.65
(95% CI, 0.55–0.77; P < .001). At the first interim analysis, T-DM1 also
demonstrated significant improvement in OS. The stratified HR for death
from any cause with T-DM1 versus lapatinib + capecitabine was 0.62
(95% CI, 0.48–0.81; P = .0005).576 Rates of grade 3 or 4 adverse events
were higher with lapatinib + capecitabine than with T-DM1 (57% vs
41%). The incidences of thrombocytopenia and increased serum
aminotransferase levels were higher with T-DM1 (frequency >25%),
whereas the incidences of diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, and
palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia were higher with lapatinib +
capecitabine.576 Therefore, the NCCN Panel notes that if the patient is
not a candidate for fam-trastuzumab nxki, then T-DM1 may be used in
the second-line setting.
Fourth-line of Therapy and Beyond for HER2-Positive Breast Cancer
Trastuzumab in combination with selected chemotherapy577 are
additional options as later lines of therapy for patients with
HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer. Randomized trials demonstrate
benefit from adding trastuzumab to other agents including paclitaxel with
or without carboplatin,577-580 docetaxel,578 and vinorelbine,578 for patients
with HER2-positive metastatic disease. The combination of trastuzumab
and capecitabine has also shown efficacy as a first-line
trastuzumab-containing regimen in this setting.581,582 The NCCN Panel
believes the 27% frequency of significant cardiac dysfunction in patients
treated with the combination of trastuzumab and
doxorubicin/cyclophosphamide chemotherapy in the metastatic setting is
too high for use of this combination outside the confines of a prospective
clinical trial.577,582,583
Lapatinib in combination with capecitabine or trastuzumab are options for
patients with HER2-positive disease following disease progression on a
trastuzumab-containing regimen.
A phase III study compared lapatinib + capecitabine with capecitabine
alone in patients with advanced or metastatic breast cancer refractory to
trastuzumab in the metastatic setting and with prior treatment with an
anthracycline and a taxane in either the metastatic or adjuvant setting.584
Time to progression was increased in the group receiving combination
therapy when compared with the group receiving capecitabine
monotherapy (8.4 vs 4.4 months; HR, 0.49; 95% CI, 0.34–0.71; P <
.001). The patients who had disease progression on monotherapy were
allowed to cross over to the combination arm. This resulted in insufficient
power to detect significant differences in OS; an exploratory analysis
demonstrated a trend toward a survival advantage with lapatinib +
capecitabine.585 The analysis reported a median OS of 75.0 weeks for
the combination arm and 64.7 weeks for the monotherapy arm (HR, 0.87;
95% CI, 0.71–1.08; P = .210).585
Results from a phase III trial in which patients with heavily pretreated
metastatic breast cancer and disease progression on trastuzumab
therapy randomly assigned to trastuzumab + lapatinib or lapatinib
monotherapy showed that PFS was increased from 8.1 to 12 weeks (P =

# MS-77

<!-- ===== Page 204 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-77
.008) with the combination.586 The OS analysis data showed that
lapatinib + trastuzumab improved median survival by 4.5 months, with
median OS of 14 months for the combination therapy and 9.5 months for
lapatinib alone (HR, 0.74; 95% CI, 0.57–0.97; P = .026). 587 This
improvement in OS analysis included patients who were initially assigned
to monotherapy and crossed over to receive combination therapy at the
time of progression.587 Based on the absence of data, the Panel does not
recommend the addition of chemotherapy to the trastuzumab and
lapatinib combination.
In a phase II trial of patients (n = 49) with progressive, HER2-positive
disease and brain metastases (92% received CNS surgery and/or
radiotherapy),588 patients were treated with capecitabine + neratinib,
a second-generation (irreversible) pan-HER tyrosine kinase inhibitor
(TKI) of the tyrosine kinase domains of EGFR, HER2, and HER4. The
patients were separated based on prior lapatinib treatment. The
combination therapy resulted in a CNS ORR of 49% (95% CI, 32%–
66%), among lapatinib-naïve patients, and 33% (95% CI, 10%–65%)
among those with prior lapatinib treatment.588 Median PFS and OS
among lapatinib-naïve patients was 5.5 and 13.3 months, and 3.1 and
15.1 months among those with prior lapatinib treatment. Grade 3
diarrhea occurred in 29% of patients.588
A prospective randomized phase III trial (NALA) randomized patients (n
= 621) with HER2-positive breast cancer to neratinib in combination with
capecitabine or lapatinib + capecitabine until disease progression.589 All
enrolled patients received a least two lines of prior HER2-targeted
treatment in the metastatic setting. Approximately 30% had received ≥3
prior treatment lines. About a third of all patients had received prior
treatment with trastuzumab, pertuzumab, and T-DM1.
The ORR (32.8% vs 26.7%; P = .1201), clinical benefit rate (44.5% vs
35.6%; P = .0328), and median DOR (8.5 vs 5.6 months) all favored the
neratinib arm. Fewer patients required intervention for CNS metastases
with neratinib. The risk of progression was reduced by 24% in the
neratinib group (HR, 0.76; 95% CI, 0.63–0.93; P = .0059). There was a
non-significant trend towards improved survival. The OS rates at 6 and
12 months were 90.2% versus 87.5% with neratinib + capecitabine
compared with 72.5% versus 66.7% for lapatinib in combination with
capecitabine (HR, 0.88; 95% CI, 0.72–1.07; P = .2086). Diarrhea was the
most frequently reported side effect in the NALA trial in both arms, but
a higher rate was observed in patients in the neratinib group (any grade
diarrhea, 83% vs 66%; grade 3/4 diarrhea, 24% vs 13%). Based on the
results of the NALA trial NCCN has included neratinib + capecitabine as
a category 2A option in this setting.
In the phase III SOPHIA trial, patients (n = 536) with disease progression
after at least two lines of anti-HER2 therapy, the addition of
margetuximab-cmkb to chemotherapy prolonged PFS compared with
trastuzumab + chemotherapy (median PFS, 5.8 vs 4.9 months; HR, 0.76;
95% CI, 0.59–0.98) whereas the OS was similar (21.6 vs 21.9
months).590,591 Serious adverse events were comparable between the
two groups. Based on the results of the SOPHIA trial, the NCCN Panel
has added chemotherapy (capecitabine, eribulin, gemcitabine, or
vinorelbine) + margetuximab-cmkb as an option in the fourth-line setting
and beyond.
Endocrine-based Therapy for HR-Positive, HER2-Postive Breast Cancer
Patients with recurrent or stage IV o HR-positive, HER2-positive tumors
have the option of receiving HER2-directed therapy as a component of
their treatment plan. Options include treatment with a HER2-targeted
therapy + cytotoxic therapy (discussed in section: Cytotoxic Therapy
HR-Positive or Negative, HER2-Positive Breast Cancer) or endocrine
therapy alone or in combination with HER2-targeted therapy.

# MS-78

<!-- ===== Page 205 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-78
Endocrine therapy in combination with HER2-targeted therapy is a less
toxic approach compared with HER2-targeted therapy combined with
chemotherapy. Premenopausal patients treated with HER2-targeted
therapy and endocrine therapy should receive ovarian suppression or
ablation. Adding trastuzumab or lapatinib to an AI has demonstrated a
PFS advantage compared with AI alone in postmenopausal patients with
stage IV or recurrent HR-positive, HER2-positive tumors.
In the TAnDEM study, postmenopausal patients (n = 207) with metastatic
HR-positive and HER2-positive tumors were randomized to receive
anastrozole alone or anastrozole + trastuzumab.592 Compared with
single-agent anastrozole, an improvement in PFS was seen with
combination therapy (4.8 vs 2.4 months; HR, 0.63; 95% CI, 0.47–0.84; P =
.0016). The combination was associated with a higher incidence of
toxicities (all grades), fatigue (21% vs 9%), diarrhea (20% vs 8%),
vomiting (21% vs 4%), and pyrexia (18% vs 7%); serious (grade 3/4)
toxicities were rare in both treatment arms.
The phase III eLEcTRA trial studied the efficacy and safety of trastuzumab
+ letrozole in patients (n = 93) with HER2-positive and HR-positive
metastatic breast cancer. Median time to disease progression was 3.3
months with letrozole and 14.1 months with trastuzumab + letrozole. The
results are consistent with the TAnDEM trial; however, due to smaller
numbers of patients enrolled in this trial, this was not statistically
significant (HR, 0.67; 95% CI, 0.35–1.29; P = .23).593
In a phase III study of postmenopausal patients (n = 219) with HER2-
positive and HR-positive disease, first-line treatment with lapatinib +
letrozole reduced the risk of disease progression compared to treatment
with letrozole alone (median PFS, 8.2 vs 3.0 months; HR, 0.71; 95% CI,
0.53–0.96; P = .019).594 The combination of letrozole + lapatanib was
associated with a higher rate of grade 3 or 4 toxicities, including diarrhea
(10% vs 1%) and rash (1% vs 0%).594
In the ALTERNATIVE trial, postmenopausal patients (n = 355) with
HER2-positive, HR-positive metastatic breast cancer were randomized to
receive lapatinib + trastuzumab + an AI, lapatinib + an AI, or trastuzumab
+ an AI without chemotherapy.595 All patients in the trial received prior
trastuzumab and prior endocrine therapy, either in the adjuvant or
metastatic disease setting. An AI in combination with lapatinib +
trastuzumab demonstrated a significant increase in PFS compared with
trastuzumab without lapatinib (11 vs 5.7 months; HR, 0.62; 95% CI, 0.45–
0.88; P = .0064).595 The most common adverse events with the
combination compared with trastuzumab or lapatinib monotherapy were
diarrhea (69%, 9%, 51%), rash (36%, 2%, 28%), nausea (22%, 9%, 22%),
The NCCN Panel has also included other combinations of available
endocrine therapies such as fulvestrant or tamoxifen with trastuzumab as
options for HR-positive and HER2-positive metastatic disease. These
options would be mostly considered after completion of chemotherapy +
HER2-therapy or in a few patients with indolent or asymptomatic disease
based on the need for continuing HER2-targeted therapy for disease
control. The selection of appropriate endocrine therapy would depend on
agents the patient has already received and paronychia (30%, 0%, 15%).
The phase II monarcHER trial data supports the use of CDK4/6 inhibitor in
combination with endocrine therapy and anti-HER2 treatment as a later
line of therapy. In this study, patients (n = 237) with unresectable locally
advanced, recurrent, or metastatic disease, who had previously
undergone at least two lines of HER2-targeted therapy—were randomized
to receive one of three regimens: abemaciclib with fulvestrant and
trastuzumab; abemaciclib with trastuzumab; or trastuzumab combined
with the physician’s choice of standard single-agent chemotherapy. After a
median follow-up of 19 months, PFS was longer in the
abemaciclib/fulvestrant/trastuzumab group (8.3 months) compared with
5.7 months in the other groups (HR, 0.67; 95% CI, 0.45–1.0, relative to

# MS-79

<!-- ===== Page 206 ===== -->

Breast Cancer
MS-79
trastuzumab + chemotherapy). The ORR was 33% in the triple-therapy
group, versus 14% in each of the two comparator arms. The data remain
immature to demonstrate OS benefit. The NCCN Panel has included
abemaciclib with fulvestrant + trastuzumab as an option for fourth line and
beyond. This is a category 2B recommendation.
Maintenance Therapy for HR-positive, HER-2 Positive Tumors: For
patients with HR-positive, HER2-positive cancers, after initial
chemotherapy with a taxane (paclitaxel or docetaxel) combined with
trastuzumab and pertuzumab, patients transition to maintenance therapy
consisting of endocrine therapy along with ongoing anti-HER2 treatment.
Adding palbociclib to the endocrine and anti-HER2 regimen is
recommended, although it is also reasonable to omit it and reserve a
CDK4/6 inhibitor for use in a later treatment line.
This strategy is supported by findings from the PATINA trial, an open-
label, international phase III study involving patients (n = 518) with HR-
positive, HER2-positive metastatic breast cancer who had no disease
progression after 4 to 8 cycles of taxane-based induction chemotherapy.571
Participants were then randomized to receive maintenance endocrine
therapy with or without palbociclib, in combination with anti-HER2 therapy
(trastuzumab with or without pertuzumab, at the physician’s discretion).
Endocrine therapy included either an AI or fulvestrant, and if
premenopausal, the patients also received ovarian suppression.571
The trial demonstrated a median PFS of 44 months in the palbociclib
group versus 29 months in the control group (HR, 0.75; 95% CI, 0.59–
0.96).571 The confirmed ORR was 29% with palbociclib compared to 22%
without it. However, grade 3 adverse events were more common in the
palbociclib arm (81% vs 31%), mainly neutropenia (56% vs 2.0%) and
leukopenia (16% vs 0.8%). Grade 4 adverse events occurred in 10% of
patients receiving palbociclib and 3.6% of those on standard therapy.571
The OS data were not yet mature.
In the randomized phase II study (PERTAIN), postmenopausal patients (n
= 258) were randomly assigned to either first-line pertuzumab +
trastuzumab and an AI (anastrozole or letrozole) or trastuzumab + an AI.
There was an improvement in PFS with the three-drug combination (18.9
vs 15.8 months; HR, 0.65; 95% CI, 0.48–0.89).596 Grade ≥3 adverse
